Analysis of anti-cancer treatment therapies based on TOPSIS method under fractional Diophantine fuzzy Muirhead mean operators
Authors: Abbas Qadir, Saleem Abdullah, Ariana Abdul Rahimzai, Saifullah Khan
Published: 2025

ABSTRACT
Cancer affects millions of lives worldwide each year, and finding the most effective treatment is a difficult and important decision for healthcare professionals. This research aims to develop a new method for selecting the most appropriate and affordable cancer treatments. For this purpose, fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets (FDFSs) and the Muirhead mean operator are introduced to deal with uncertainty in medical decision-making. A difficult problem in decision-making is to detect the hidden weight information of criteria and decision-making experts. To solve this problem, the analytical hierarchy process (AHP) is used to calculate unknown weights for both criteria and decision-making experts. Furthermore, a fractional Diophantine fuzzy-TOPSIS (FDF-TOPSIS) technique is proposed to evaluate and select the best treatment process. Next, the proposed FDF-TOPSIS technique is applied to a real-life numerical example of finding the optimal cancer treatment process. Finally, we compare our proposed method with other existing methods and demonstrate the reliability, accuracy, and feasibility of the proposed technique, highlighting its potential to improve decision-making during cancer treatment.

Introduction

Cancer is a critical public health problem all over the globe. It is an abnormal cell that divides uncontrollably, it begins when genetic changes cause one cell to expand and multiply quickly. These cells might eventually invade different tissues. Global demographic trends indicate a rise in cancer incidence over the coming decades, with 420 million new cases annually anticipated by 2025, indicating a significant public health issue1. To address this problem, various treatments have been developed, such as radiation therapy, chemotherapy, hadron therapy, surgery, hormone therapy, and immunotherapy2. The main variables that influence treatment decisions for a given cancer are patient, disease, and treatment-related factors3. Other factors that play a significant role in the course of treatment, such as treatment duration, cost, comfort, side effects, and survival rate percentage, must also be considered to evaluate the various treatment options. However, it is frequently difficult for crisp sets to properly handle these types of issues. In light of this, Zadeh4 defined the fuzzy set (FS) by referring to a Positive membership degree (PMD) lying in a unit interval. Relative to conventional mathematical binary representations, FS is a more significant idea. However, this concept cannot properly handle the real-life problem. Thus, in 1986, Attonossov5 developed the theory of IFS by incorporating a negative membership degree (NMD) into the FS theory that satisfies the necessary conditions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcal{M}+\mathcal{N}\le\:1$$\end{document}. IFS is a strong tool that defines human perspectives as yes or no. IFS has received significant attention from scholars during the past years6. Peide, and Dengfeng defined Muirhead mean operators for IFS7. There are some cases in decision-making, i.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcal{M}+\mathcal{N}>1$$\end{document} which cannot be handled by IFS. Thus, Yager8 presented a new theory called Pythagorean fuzzy set (PyFS), a generalized version of IFS that complies with the requirements \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}^{2}+{\mathcal{N}}^{2}\le\:1$$\end{document}. Liu et al.9 explored PyF Muirhead mean operators. In many cases, the condition of PyFS failed hence for describing fuzziness Yager10 presented a q-rung orthopair fuzzy set (q-ROFS) which is a generalized version of IFS and PyFS. A variety of different aggregation operators Heronian mean (HM)11,12, Maclaurin symmetric mean (MSM)13, and partitioned MSM14 operators have been offered for q-ROFNs. Later, Saleem et al.,15 defined an extended concept that he named a fractional fuzzy set (FFSs) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}^{f}+{\mathcal{N}}^{f}\le\:1$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:f=\frac{p}{q},q\ne\:0$$\end{document}. FFSs are an updated version, so Saleem et al.15 discussed their special cases and also explored their application. After the invention of FFS, Qiyas et al.16 defined fractional orthotriple fuzzy rough sets. FFS is an effective generalization that offers more flexibility for applications in practical settings. Subsequently, Riaz and Hashmi17 offered the Linear Diophantine Fuzzy Set (LDFS), by incorporating reference parameters (RPs) into MD and NMD. In LDFS, has three membership degrees: membership \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcal{M}$$\end{document}, non-membership \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcal{N}$$\end{document}, and reference parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{A}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{M}$$\end{document} that correspond to the requirements \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{A}\mathcal{M}+\mathfrak{M}\mathcal{N}\le\:1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{A}+\mathfrak{M}\le\:1$$\end{document}. In the LDF scenario, TOPSIS was recently introduced by Gull and Advogado18. In the context of a Linear Diophantine Fuzzy Soft Set, Ibtesam Alshammari devised the topsis approach19. Many scholars have focused on investigating LDFSs and decision-making because LDFNs give greater freedom to decision-makers. But in some real-world scenarios, the requirements for RPs are often more than one. Because of this problem, Almagrabi et al.20 defined q-rung linear Diophantine fuzzy sets (q-RLDFS) by specifying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}^{q}\mathcal{M}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{q}\mathcal{N}\le\:1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}^{q}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{q}\le\:1$$\end{document}. The main advantage of the q-RLDF approach is that it considers the qth power of RPs, signifying that it is appropriate for dealing with real-world DM difficulties. H. KamacÄs introduced the notion of Complex LDFSs (CLDFSs)21 in 2022. Ali et al., defined Heronian operators for CLDFSs22. Iampan et al.,23 suggested Einstein aggregation operations for LDFSs. Ayub et al.,24 studied and described generalized LDFS and invented its relation. Farid et al.,25 studied the spherical fuzzy set and LDFSs and defined a hybrid structure of spherical linear Diophantine fuzzy sets. Quran26 extended LDFS to T-spherical linear Diophantine fuzzy sets (T-SLDFSs). In 2022, Petchimuthu et al.27 put forward LDFSs to an interval-valued linear Diophantine fuzzy set and defined some useful aggregation operators. In 2023, Ashraf et al.28 generalized SLDFS and defined spherical q-linear Diophantine fuzzy set (Sq-LDFS) and also explored its significant aspects. Ali, J.29 proposed T-spherical fuzzy aczel-alsina prioritized aggregation operators for solving decision-making problems. In different environments, authors defined some different aggregation operators and applied them to decision-making problems30. Riaz et al.31 developed FDF aggregation operators and decision-making processes. Phakakorn, and Pairote32 developed the mathematical structure of (p, q)-rung linear Diophantine fuzzy set and also discussed its application.

Although many different decision-making methods have been presented in the last few decades, the TOPSIS method has become one of the most widely used and successful techniques used in many areas of decision-making in various ambiguous environments. It is also important to note that a drawback of current TOPSIS methods is that they only use known decision-maker (DMs) weights, known criterion weights, or both when solving decision-making challenges. Several studies worked with unknown weight data of DMs, while criteria weights have been identified. On the other hand, some scholars have developed the TOPSIS method to deal with MCGDM, where the decision-maker weight is known, but the criterion weight is unknown. Therefore, there is no TOPSIS method under fractional Diophantine fuzzy from the existing literature to solve the situation of MCGDM where the criteria and decision-maker weights are completely unknown.

Motivation of paper

During the available research review, the authors discovered the absence of the technique and application of the TOPSIS method with hybrid structures of fractional fuzzy sets and Diophantine fuzzy sets, where the criteria and associated weights of the experts are completely uncertain. As a result of the above discussion, the present study proposes the TOPSIS technique in a fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets environment.

The motivation for this study is given below.

Many recent studies on Diophantine fuzzy sets are insufficient and their properties have not been fully explored. Therefore, it is necessary to develop an innovative idea fractional Diophantine fuzzy set, which has a wide range to evaluate decision information more accurately.Decision-making involves different types of weight information such as known and unknown weight vectors. In the case of unknown weights, it is very difficult for decision makers to draw a successful result which is a difficult task. To solve this challenge, an entropy measure based on FDFSs must be defined to determine the unknown weight information.In a fractional Diophantine fuzzy environment, traditional decision-making operators are unable to capture the relationship between criteria. To overcome these challenges, Muirhead Mean operators that accurately capture the relationship between criteria must be designed in a fractional Diophantine fuzzy environment.The TOPSIS method is an effective tool for choosing the optimal option based on the smallest distance to the positive ideal solution (PIS) and the greatest distance to the negative ideal solution (NIS). Thus a TOPSIS technique should be developed in the context of fractional Diophantine fuzzy information to select the best alternative.As a research focus on MCGDM issues, the selection of a treatment process for a cancer patient is an important area of research. Therefore, this paper focuses on the TOPSIS method for the selection of a treatment process for a cancer patient.

To solve this challenge, an entropy measure based on FDFSs must be defined to determine the unknown weight information.

Contribution of the paper

The main focus of this research is to find an effective treatment for cancer patients, which is a very difficult task in real-life problems. This research study has contributed to the analysis of multi criteria group decision making (MCGDM) under ambiguity in the following manner: We design a new operation for fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets to handle the decision issues characterized by fractional Diophantine fuzzy information.Muirhead Mean operators with flexible operational parameters have great significance as they incorporate the properties of several others. Therefore, to aggregate the fractional Diophantine fuzzy information we introduce a variety of aggregation operators such as the fractional linear Diophantine fuzzy Muirhead Mean (FLDFMM), fractional Diophantine fuzzy weighted Muirhead Mean (FDFWMM), fractional Diophantine fuzzy Dual Muirhead Mean (FDFDWMM) and fractional Diophantine fuzzy Dual weighted Muirhead Mean (FDFDWMM) operator.A fractional linear Diophantine fuzzy -TOPSIS method is developed to handle situations such as unknown weight information. In real-life medical decision-making, there are many factors involved that need to be handled with more flexibility than other fuzzy sets. Consequently, the TOPSIS approach under fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets allows for more accurate modeling of uncertainties and imperfections that traditional fuzzy sets are unable to handle adequately.A novel analytical hierarchy process (AHP) is proposed for finding the criteria and the expert’s weight under fractional Diophantine fuzzy information. AHP is a powerful method and can handle complex decisions when calculating criteria weights for different decision-making frameworks.

We design a new operation for fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets to handle the decision issues characterized by fractional Diophantine fuzzy information.

We design a new operation for fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets to handle the decision issues characterized by fractional Diophantine fuzzy information.

Muirhead Mean operators with flexible operational parameters have great significance as they incorporate the properties of several others. Therefore, to aggregate the fractional Diophantine fuzzy information we introduce a variety of aggregation operators such as the fractional linear Diophantine fuzzy Muirhead Mean (FLDFMM), fractional Diophantine fuzzy weighted Muirhead Mean (FDFWMM), fractional Diophantine fuzzy Dual Muirhead Mean (FDFDWMM) and fractional Diophantine fuzzy Dual weighted Muirhead Mean (FDFDWMM) operator.

A fractional linear Diophantine fuzzy -TOPSIS method is developed to handle situations such as unknown weight information. In real-life medical decision-making, there are many factors involved that need to be handled with more flexibility than other fuzzy sets. Consequently, the TOPSIS approach under fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets allows for more accurate modeling of uncertainties and imperfections that traditional fuzzy sets are unable to handle adequately.

Consequently, the TOPSIS approach under fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets allows for more accurate modeling of uncertainties and imperfections that traditional fuzzy sets are unable to handle adequately.

A novel analytical hierarchy process (AHP) is proposed for finding the criteria and the expert’s weight under fractional Diophantine fuzzy information. AHP is a powerful method and can handle complex decisions when calculating criteria weights for different decision-making frameworks.

Rest of paper. Section 1, is about basic definition. Section 2 contain a novel theory of fractional Diophantine fuzzy set and Murhid mean operators. In Sect. 3 a novel TOPSIS method is developed for fractional Diophantine fuzzy set and numerical application proposed method. At last, a comparative analysis and conclusion are given.

Preliminaries

In this section, we review some basic concepts related to IFSs, PyFSs, LDFSs, and q-RLDFS that will be employed in the next sections.

Definition 5

Ref.17 For each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:x\in\:\mathcal{Z}\ne\:0$$\end{document}, linear Diophantine fuzzy set (LDFS) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:$$\end{document} is mathematically given as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:=\left\{\left\{x,{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}\left(x\right),{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}\left(x\right)\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:},{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}\right\}|x\in\:\mathcal{Z}\right\}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}:z\to\:\left[\text{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{P}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}:z\to\:\left[\text{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{N}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}$$\end{document} is the reference perimeter lie between unit interval restricted to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{A}{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}+\mathfrak{M}{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}\le\:1,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}\le\:1$$\end{document}

Definition 6

Ref.20 For a fixed set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcal{Z}$$\end{document}, the q-linear Diophantine fuzzy set (q-LDFS) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:$$\end{document} is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:=\left\{\left\{x,{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}\left(x\right),{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}\left(x\right)\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:},{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}\right\}|x\in\:\mathcal{Z}\right\}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}:z\to\:\left[\text{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{P}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}:z\to\:\left[\text{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{N}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}$$\end{document} is the reference perimeter lie between unit interval restricted to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:}^{q}{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}^{q}{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}\le\:1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:}^{q}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}^{q}\le\:1$$\end{document}

Definition 7

Ref.32 Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcal{Z}$$\end{document} be a non-empty set. Then for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:x\in\:\mathcal{Z}$$\end{document} the (p, q)-linear Diophantine fuzzy set ((p, q)-LDFS) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:$$\end{document} is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:=\left\{\left\{x,{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}\left(x\right),{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}\left(x\right)\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:},{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}\right\}|x\in\:\mathcal{Z}\right\}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}:z\to\:\left[\text{0,1}\right]$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}:z\to\:\left[\text{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{P}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{N}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}$$\end{document} is the reference perimeter lie between unit interval restricted to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:}^{p}{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}^{q}{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}\le\:1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:}^{p}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}^{q}\le\:1$$\end{document}.

Fractional Diophantine fuzzy set (FDFS)

This section provides a theory of fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets and their basic properties that are very useful for dealing with uncertainty.

Definition 8

For any fixed set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcal{Z}$$\end{document}, the fractional Diophantine fuzzy set (FDFS) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:$$\end{document} is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:=\left\{\left\{x,{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}\left(x\right),{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}\left(x\right)\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:},{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}\right\}|x\in\:\mathcal{Z}\right\}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}:z\to\:\left[\text{0,1}\right]$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}:z\to\:\left[\text{0,1}\right]$$\end{document} is named as positive membership degree ( \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{P}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} ) and negative membership degree ( \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{N}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} ), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}$$\end{document} is the reference perimeter lie between unit interval restricted to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:}^{f}{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}^{f}{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}\le\:1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:}^{f}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}^{f}\le\:1$$\end{document}. Where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:f=\frac{p}{q},\left(p,q\in\:z,\:\frac{p}{q}\ge\:1,q\ne\:0\right).$$\end{document} Simply the fractional Diophantine fuzzy set is represented by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:=\left\{\left\{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\right\},\left\{\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}.

There are some cases of fractional Diophantine fuzzy sets as follows.

Case 1

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:f=\frac{p}{q}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p=q$$\end{document}, the fractional Diophantine fuzzy set is reduced to a linear Diophantine fuzzy set.

Case 2

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:f=\frac{p}{q}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p=3q$$\end{document}, the fractional Diophantine fuzzy set is reduced to a q-non linear Diophantine fuzzy set.

Example 1

Let us \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}_{\wp\:}=1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{N}}_{\wp\:}=0.87$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{\wp\:}=0.75,$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{M}}_{\wp\:}=0.9$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(1\right)\left(0.75\right)\:+\:\left(0.87\right)\left(0.9\right)\:>\:1$$\end{document} which contradicts the condition of LDFS, by setting q = 3 on the RPs (e.g. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(1\right){\left(0.75\right)}^{3}++\:\left(0.87\right){\left(0.9\right)}^{3}<1,\:$$\end{document}which contradicts the condition of q-LDFS, now by setting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:f=\frac{p}{q}$$\end{document}, p=8,q=2, on the RPs (e.g. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(1\right){\left(0.75\right)}^{\frac{8}{2}}++\:\left(0.87\right){\left(0.9\right)}^{\frac{8}{2}}<1\:)$$\end{document}, where 0.75; 0.9 is RPs, for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{P}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{N}\text{M}\text{D}$$\end{document} respectively which fulfill the condition of fractional Diophantine fuzzy set. That’s why we need a fractional Diophantine fuzzy set, which can handle the limitation of LDFS and q-LDFS condition with the help of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:f=\frac{p}{q}$$\end{document} power of RPs. As discussed above, by converting the power from natural numbers to rational numbers, we developed a useful generalization that allows us to easily transform the proposed tool to existential concepts such as LDFSs, and q-LDFSs. The summary of the above definition is given in (Fig. 2):

Fig. 2Summary of the above definitions.

Summary of the above definitions.

Definition 9

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:=\left\{\left\{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\right\},\left\{\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document} be the fractional Diophantine fuzzy number (FDFNs) then the score and accuracy function are of the form\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{S}\left(\wp\:\right)=\frac{\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{N}+{\mathfrak{A}}^{f}-{\mathfrak{M}}^{f}}{2}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{C}\left(\wp\:\right)=\frac{\mathcal{M}+\mathcal{N}}{4}+\frac{{\mathfrak{A}}^{f}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{f}}{2}$$\end{document}

Definition 10

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\wp\:=\left\{\left\{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\right\},\left\{\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{P}=\left\{\left\{\dot{\mathcal{M}},\dot{\mathcal{N}}\right\},\left\{\dot{\mathfrak{A}},\dot{\mathfrak{M}}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document} be the two fractional linear Diophantine fuzzy number then,

1). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{\wp\:}\oplus\:\mathfrak{P}=\left\{\left\{\sqrt[f]{{\mathcal{M}}^{f}+{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}^{f}-{\mathcal{M}}^{f}.{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}^{f}},{\mathcal{N}}^{f}.{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}^{f}\right\},\left\{\sqrt[f]{{\mathfrak{A}}^{f}+{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}^{f}-{\mathfrak{A}}^{f}.{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}^{f}},{\mathfrak{M}}^{f}.{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}^{f}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

2). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{\wp\:}\otimes\:\mathfrak{P}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}^{f}.{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}^{f},\sqrt[f]{{\mathcal{N}}^{f}+{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}^{f}-{\mathcal{N}}^{f}.{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}^{f}}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}^{f}.{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}^{f},\sqrt[f]{{\mathfrak{M}}^{f}+{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}^{f}-{\mathfrak{M}}^{f}.{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}^{f}}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

3). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\gamma\:.\wp\:=\left\{\left\{\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}^{f}\right)}^{\gamma\:}},{\mathcal{N}}^{\gamma\:}\right\},\left\{\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}^{f}\right)}^{\gamma\:}},{\mathfrak{M}}^{\gamma\:}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

4). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}^{\gamma\:}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}^{\gamma\:},\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}^{f}\right)}^{\gamma\:}}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\gamma\:},\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}^{f}\right)}^{\gamma\:}}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

Muirhead mean operator

Definition 11

Ref.33 For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:i=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n$$\end{document} let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}$$\end{document} be a collection of non-negative real numbers with parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},{\mathcalligra{p}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document}. The Muirhead Mean (MM) operator is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{MM}^{\acute{\rm P}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\:{\left(\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.\left({\oplus\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({\otimes\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\wp\:}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right)$$\end{document} is any permutation and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{S}}_{n}$$\end{document} is the collection of permutation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right).$$\end{document} By assigning some special vectors to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}$$\end{document}, we can obtain some special cases of the MM.

1). When \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right),$$\end{document} then the MM is called an arithmetic averaging operator as follows

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{MM}^{\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\frac{1}{n}{\sum\:}_{J=1}^{n}{\wp\:}_{j}$$\end{document}.

2). When \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n$}\right.,\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n$}\right.,\dots\:,\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n$}\right.\right),$$\end{document} then the MM operator will become geometric averaging as follows

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{MM}^{\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n},\dots\:,\frac{1}{n}\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)={\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left({\wp\:}_{j}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}.$$\end{document}

3). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\text{1,1},\text{0,0},\dots\:,0\right),$$\end{document} then the MM is said to be the BM operator34

operator as follows.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{MM}^{\left(\text{1,1},\text{0,0},\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)={\left(\frac{1}{n\left(n-1\right)}{\sum\:}_{\begin{array}{c}i,j=1\\\:i\ne\:j\end{array}}^{n}{\wp\:}_{i}{\wp\:}_{j}\right)}^{\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}

4). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcalligra{p} = \overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}}$$\end{document}, then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:MM$$\end{document} is called Maclaurin symmetric operators35 as follows

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$MM^{{\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}}}} \left( {\wp _{1} ,\wp _{2} , \ldots ,\wp _{n} } \right) = \left( {\frac{{\begin{array}{*{20}c} \oplus \\ {1 \le i_{1} < \ldots < i_{k} \le n} \\ \end{array} \oplus _{{j = 1}}^{k} \wp _{{ij}} }}{{C_{n}^{k} }}~} \right)$$\end{document}

Fractional Diophantine fuzzy Muirhead mean operator

In decision making various types of aggregation operators are used but they are unable to accurately capture the uncertainty. Therefore, here we developed the Muirhead Mean operator for fractional Diophantine fuzzy set which generalizes several classical means, namely harmonic, geometric and arithmetic means. The Muirhead operator provides additional flexibility in combining and aggregating data from different sources, taking into account the various connections between criteria. Muirhead operators improve the accuracy and reliability of decision models, they can be used to make various real-world decisions.

Definition 12

Let us have a collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}\:\left(i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right)$$\end{document} with parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document} and permutation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right),$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right),$$\end{document} then the fractional linear Diophantine fuzzy Muirhead Mean (FLDFMM) operator is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\:{\left(\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.\left({\oplus\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({\otimes\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\wp\:}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{S}}_{n}$$\end{document} is the collection of permutation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right),$$\end{document} we can get the aggregation result described as Theorem 3.

Theorem 1

Let we have a collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy number \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} Proof

\setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} Theorem 2

Let us have a collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}\:\left(i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right)$$\end{document} with parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document} , then the basic properties of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator are as follows.

Idempotency

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}=\wp\:=\left\{\left\{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\right\},\left\{\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}, then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\wp\:$$\end{document}

Proof

As for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}=\wp\:=\left\{\left\{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\right\},\left\{\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}, by theorem 1 as according to the proposed \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} aggregation operator\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{\begin{array}{c}{\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}},\\\:\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule Monotonicity

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:i,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i},{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i},{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} be the FDFNs, such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i}\ge\:\mathcal{M}}_{i},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}_{i}\le\:\mathcal{N}}_{i},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i}\ge\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\le\:\mathfrak{M}}_{i},$$\end{document} then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)\le\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\dot{\wp\:}}_{1},{\dot{\wp\:}}_{2},\dots\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{n}\right).$$\end{document}

Proof

We know that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{i}$$\end{document} are the FDFNs.

Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\le\:{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i}$$\end{document} then we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\le\:{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\le\:{\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f},$$\end{document} further\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\le\:1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)\le\:{\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)$$\end{document} and

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\le\:{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}$$\end{document}implies that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\le\:\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}$$\end{document}

Hence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\le\:{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i}.$$\end{document} By the same method, we can prove that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i}\ge\:{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\le\:\mathfrak{M}}_{i}.$$\end{document} There are some special cases discussed as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} Boundedness

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{+}=\left\{\left\{\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right)\right\},\left\{\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right)\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{-}=\left\{\left\{\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right)\right\},\left\{\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right)\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{+}\le\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)\le\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{-}.$$\end{document}

As it relates to the parameter vector, we shall examine a few specific uses of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator in the following.

1). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right),$$\end{document} then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called fractional Diophantine fuzzy arithmetic averaging operators as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}{MM}^{\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\frac{1}{n}{\sum\:}_{J=1}^{n}{\wp\:}_{j}=\left\{\left\{\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}},{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{1/n}\right\},\left\{\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}},\:{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{1/n}\:\right\}\right.$$\end{document}

2). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\text{1,1},1,\dots\:,1\right),$$\end{document} then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called fractional Diophantine fuzzy geometric averaging operators as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)={\left({\sum\:}_{J=1}^{n}{\wp\:}_{j}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}=\left\{\left\{{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{1/n},\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}}\right\},\left\{{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{1/n},\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}}\right\}\right.$$\end{document}

3). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\lambda\:,\text{0,0},\dots\:,0\right),$$\end{document} then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called fractional Diophantine fuzzy generalized averaging operators as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}{MM}^{\left(\lambda\:,\text{0,0},\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)={\left(\frac{1}{n}{\sum\:}_{J=1}^{n}{\wp\:}_{j}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}}=\left\{\begin{array}{c}\left\{{\left(\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{f\lambda\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}},\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left({1-\left(1-{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{\lambda\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}}}\right\},\\\:\left\{{\left(\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{f\lambda\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}},\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left({1-\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{\lambda\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}}}\right\}\end{array}\right.$$\end{document}

4). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}},$$\end{document} then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called Maclaurin symmetric operators as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} FDFMM^{{\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{ \ldots , \ldots , \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}}}} \ldots 1,\wp _{2} , \ldots ,\wp _{n} & = \left( {\frac{{\begin{array}{*{20}c} \oplus \\ {1 \le i_{1} < \ldots < i_{k} \le n} \\ \end{array} \oplus _{{j = 1}}^{k} \wp _{{ij}} }}{{C_{n}^{k} }}~} \right) \\ & = \left\{ {\left\{ {\begin{array}{*{20}c} {\sqrt[f]{{\left( {1 - \mathop \prod \limits_{{1 \le i_{1} < \ldots < i_{k} \le n}} \left( {1 - \mathop \prod \limits_{{j = 1}}^{k} {\mathcal{M}}_{{ij}}^{f} } \right)^{{\frac{1}{{C_{n}^{k} }}}} } \right)^{{\frac{1}{k}}} }}} \\ {\sqrt[f]{{1 - \left( {1 - \left( {\mathop \prod \limits_{{1 \le i_{1} < \ldots < i_{k} \le n}} \left( {1 - \mathop \prod \limits_{{j = 1}}^{k} \left( {1 - {\mathcal{N}}_{{ij}}^{f} } \right)} \right)^{{\frac{1}{{C_{n}^{k} }}}} } \right)} \right)^{{\frac{1}{k}}} }}} \\ \end{array} } \right\}} \right. \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left.\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{{\left(1-\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}<\dots\:<{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}{\mathfrak{A}}_{ij}^{f}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}},\\\:\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-\left(\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}<\dots\:<{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{ij}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}}\end{array}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

5). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n},\dots\:,\frac{1}{n}\right),$$\end{document} then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called fractional Diophantine fuzzy geometric averaging operator as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{L}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n},\dots\:,\frac{1}{n}\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)={\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left({\wp\:}_{j}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}==\left\{\left\{{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{1/n},\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}}\right\},\left\{{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{1/n},\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}}\right\}\right.$$\end{document}

Fractional Diophantine fuzzy weighted Muirhead mean operator

Definition 13

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\},i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right\}$$\end{document} be the collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy number, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{f}=\left({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathfrak{f}}_{n}\right)$$\end{document} be the weight vectors such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}>0,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\sum\:_{i=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}=1,$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},{\mathcalligra{p}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document} be a parameter vector, then the fractional Diophantine fuzzy weighted Muirhead Mean (FDFWMM) operator is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\:{\left(\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.{\oplus\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}{\otimes\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left({\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}{\wp\:}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}$$\end{document}
