where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right),$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right)$$\end{document} is any permutation and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{S}}_{n}$$\end{document} is the collection of permutation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right),$$\end{document} we can get the aggregation result described as Theorem 3.

Theorem 3

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\},i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right\}$$\end{document} be the collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy number, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{f}=\left({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathfrak{f}}_{n}\right)$$\end{document} be the weight vectors such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}>0$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\sum\:_{i=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}=1$$\end{document} , and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},{\mathcalligra{p}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document}be a parameter vector, then by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator the aggregated result is still fractional Diophantine fuzzy number as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}{c}\left\{\begin{array}{c}{\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\:\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}},\\\:\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\left({\mathcal{N}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\:\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\:}\right)\end{array}\right\},\\\:\left\{\begin{array}{c}{\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\:\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}},\\\:\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\:\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\:}\right)\end{array}\right\}\end{array}\right\}$$\end{document}

Proof

Same as theorem 1.

Theorem 4

Suppose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\},i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right\}$$\end{document} be the collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy number, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{f}=\left({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathfrak{f}}_{n}\right)$$\end{document} be the weight vectors such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}>0$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\sum\:_{i=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}=1$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},{\mathcalligra{p}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document} be a parameter vector, then the basic properties of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator are as follows.

Monotonicity

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:i,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i},{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i},{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} be the FLDFNs, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i}<\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}_{i}\ge\:{\mathcal{N}}_{i},{\mathfrak{A}}_{i}>{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\ge\:{\mathfrak{M}}_{i},$$\end{document} then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)\le\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\dot{\wp\:}}_{1},{\dot{\wp\:}}_{2},\dots\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{n}\right).$$\end{document}

Proof

Same as theorem 2.

Boundedness

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{+}=\left\{\left\{\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right)\right\},\left\{\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right)\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{-}=\left\{\left\{\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right)\right\},\left\{\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right)\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{+}\le\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)\le\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{-}.$$\end{document}

As it relates to the parameter vector, we shall examine a few specific uses of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator in the following.

1). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right),$$\end{document} then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{W}{MM}^{\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}/n}},{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}/n}\right\},\left\{\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}/n}},\:{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}/n}\:\right\}\right.$$\end{document}

2). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}},$$\end{document} then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\text{FDFW}}MM^{{\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}}}} \left( {\wp _{1} ,\wp _{2} , \ldots ,\wp _{n} } \right)=\left\{\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{{\left(1-\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}\dots\:{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}{\left({1-\mathcal{M}}_{ij}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}},\\\:\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-\left(\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}\dots\:{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}\left(1-{\mathcal{N}}_{ij}^{f{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}}\end{array}\right\},\right.$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left.\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{{\left(1-\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}<\dots\:<{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}{\left({1-\mathfrak{A}}_{ij}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}},\\\:\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-\left(\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}<\dots\:<{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{ij}^{f{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}}\end{array}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

Fractional Diophantine fuzzy dual Muirhead mean operator

Definition 14

Let us have a collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}\:\left(i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right)$$\end{document} with a set of parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document} and permutation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right),$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right),$$\end{document} then the fractional Diophantine fuzzy Dual Muirhead Mean (FLDFDMM) operator is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\:{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\left({\oplus\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({\otimes\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}{\wp\:}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{S}}_{n}$$\end{document} is the collection of permutation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right),$$\end{document} we can get the aggregation result described as Theorem 5.

Theorem 5

Let we have a collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy number \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}\:\left(i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right)$$\end{document} with a set of parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document}, then by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator the aggregated result is still fractional Diophantine fuzzy number as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}{c}\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}},}\\\:{\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{N}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\end{array}\right\},\\\:\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}},\\\:{\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\end{array}\right\}\end{array}\right\}$$\end{document}

Theorem 6

Let us have a collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}\:\left(i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right)$$\end{document} with a set of parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document}, then the basic properties of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator are as follows.

Idempotency

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}=\wp\:=\left\{\left\{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\right\},\left\{\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{M}\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\wp\:$$\end{document}

Monotonicity

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:i,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i},{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i},{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} be the FLDFNs, when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i}<\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}_{i}\ge\:{\mathcal{N}}_{i},{\mathfrak{A}}_{i}>{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\ge\:{\mathfrak{M}}_{i},$$\end{document} then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)\le\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\dot{\wp\:}}_{1},{\dot{\wp\:}}_{2},\dots\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{n}\right).$$\end{document}

Boundedness

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{+}=\left\{\left\{\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right)\right\},\left\{\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right)\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{-}=\left\{\left\{\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right)\right\},\left\{\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right)\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{+}\le\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)\le\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{-}.$$\end{document}

As it relates to the parameter vector, we shall examine a few specific uses of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator in the following.

1). When the value of parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right),$$\end{document} then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called fractional Diophantine fuzzy geometric averaging operators as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{1/n},\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}}\right\},\left\{{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{1/n},\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}}\right\}\right.$$\end{document}

2). When the value of parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\lambda\:,\text{0,0},\dots\:,0\right),$$\end{document} then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is named fractional Diophantine fuzzy generalized geometric operators as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\:\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\left(\lambda\:,\text{0,0},\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}{c}\left\{\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left({1-\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{\lambda\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}}},{\left(\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{f\lambda\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}}\right\},\\\:\left\{\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left({1-\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{\lambda\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}}},{\left(\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{f\lambda\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{n}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{\lambda\:}}\right\}\end{array}\right\}$$\end{document}

3). When the value of parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}}$$\end{document}, then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called Maclaurin symmetric operators as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-\left(\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}\dots\:{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{ij}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}},\\\:\sqrt[f]{{\left(1-\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}\dots\:{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}{\mathcal{N}}_{ij}^{f}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}}\end{array}\right\}\right.$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left.\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-\left(\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}<\dots\:<{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{ij}^{f}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}},\\\:\sqrt[f]{{\left(1-\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}<\dots\:<{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}{\mathfrak{M}}_{ij}^{f}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}}\end{array}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

4). When the value of parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\text{1,1},1,\dots\:,1\right),$$\end{document} then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called fractional Diophantine fuzzy averaging operators as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\left(\text{1,1},1,\dots\:,1\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}},{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{1/n}\right\},\left\{\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}},{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{1/n}\right\}\right.$$\end{document}

5). When \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n},\dots\:,\frac{1}{n}\right),$$\end{document} then the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} is called fractional Diophantine fuzzy averaging operator as follows

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}MM}^{\left(\frac{1}{n},\frac{1}{n},\dots\:,\frac{1}{n}\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}},{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{1/n}\right\},\left\{\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{1/n}},{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{1/n}\right\}\right.$$\end{document}

Fractional Diophantine fuzzy dual weighted Muirhead mean operator

Definition 16

Let we have a fractional Diophantine fuzzy number collection \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\},i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right\},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{f}=\left({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathfrak{f}}_{n}\right)$$\end{document} be the weight vectors such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}>0,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\sum\:_{i=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}=1,$$\end{document} with parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},{\mathcalligra{p}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n},$$\end{document} then the fractional Diophantine fuzzy dual weighted Muirhead Mean (FDFDWMM) operator is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\:{\left({\otimes\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left({\oplus\:}_{j=1}^{n}{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}\left({\wp\:}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right),$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right)$$\end{document} is any permutation and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{S}}_{n}$$\end{document} is the collection of permutation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left(j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right),$$\end{document} we can get the aggregation result described as Theorem 7.

Theorem 7

Let we have a fractional Diophantine fuzzy number collection \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\},i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right\}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathfrak{f}=\left({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathfrak{f}}_{n}\right)$$\end{document} be the weight vectors such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}>0$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\sum\:_{i=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}=1$$\end{document}, with parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},{\mathcalligra{p}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document}, then the fractional Diophantine fuzzy dual weighted Muirhead Mean (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document}) operator is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\left({\mathcal{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\:\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\:}\right),\\\:{\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\left(1-{\mathcal{N}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\:\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\end{array}\right\}\right.$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left.\left\{\begin{array}{c}\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\:\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\:}\right),\\\:{\left(\sqrt[f]{1-{\left({\prod\:}_{{\Theta\:}\hbox{c}{\kern-6.5pt}\raise2.75pt\hbox{\vrule height .5pt width3.5pt depth0pt}{\kern4pt}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}}\left(1-\left({\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\left(1-{\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\Theta\:}\left(j\right)}}\right)}^{{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}\right)\right)\right)}^{\raisebox{1ex}{$1$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$n!$}\right.}\:\:}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcalligra{p}}_{j}}}\end{array}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

Theorem 8

Let us have a collection of fractional Diophantine fuzzy numbers \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}\:\left(i=\text{1,2},\dots\:n\right)$$\end{document} with a set of parameter vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left({\mathcalligra{p}}_{1},\dots\:,{\mathcalligra{p}}_{n}\right)\in\:{R}^{n}$$\end{document}, then the basic properties of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document} operator are as follows.

Monotonicity

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:i,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\mathfrak{A}}_{i},{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{i}=\left\{\left\{{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i},{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}_{i}\right\},\left\{{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i},{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} be the FDFNs, when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\dot{\mathcal{M}}}_{i}<\mathcal{M}}_{i},{\dot{\mathcal{N}}}_{i}\ge\:{\mathcal{N}}_{i},{\mathfrak{A}}_{i}>{\dot{\mathfrak{A}}}_{i},$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\dot{\mathfrak{M}}}_{i}\ge\:{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}$$\end{document} then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)\le\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\dot{\wp\:}}_{1},{\dot{\wp\:}}_{2},\dots\:{\dot{\wp\:}}_{n}\right)$$\end{document}.

Proof

Same as theorem 2.

Boundedness

Suppose for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:,$$\end{document} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{+}=\left\{\left\{\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right)\right\},\left\{\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right)\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{-}=\left\{\left\{\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathcal{N}}_{i}\right)\right\},\left\{\underset{i}{\text{min}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{i}\right),\underset{i}{\text{max}}\left({\mathfrak{M}}_{i}\right)\right\}\right\},$$\end{document} then

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{+}\le\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:{\wp\:}_{n}\right)\le\:{\wp\:}_{i}^{-}.$$\end{document}

As it relates to the parameter vector, we shall examine a few specific uses of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}MM}^{\mathcalligra{p}}$$\end{document}operator in the following.

1). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right),$$\end{document} then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}{MM}^{\left(\text{1,0},0,\dots\:,0\right)}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{M}}_{j}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}/n},\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}/n}}\right\},\left\{{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}{\mathfrak{A}}_{j}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}/n},\sqrt[f]{{1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-{\mathfrak{M}}_{j}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}/n}}\:\right\}\right.$$\end{document}

2). If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\mathcalligra{p}=\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}},$$\end{document} then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{F}\text{D}\text{F}\text{D}\text{W}{MM}^{\overbrace {{1,1, \ldots ,1}}^{k},\overbrace {{0,0, \ldots ,0}}^{{n - k}}}\left({\wp\:}_{1},{\wp\:}_{2},\dots\:,{\wp\:}_{n}\right)=\left\{\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-\left(\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}\dots\:{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}\left(1-{\mathcal{M}}_{ij}^{f{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}},\\\:\sqrt[f]{{\left(1-\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}\dots\:{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}{\left({1-\mathcal{N}}_{ij}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}}\end{array}\right\},\right.$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\left.\left\{\begin{array}{c}\sqrt[f]{1-{\left(1-\left(\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}<\dots\:<{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}\left(1-{\mathfrak{A}}_{ij}^{f{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}},\\\:\sqrt[f]{{\left(1-\prod\:_{1\le\:{i}_{1}<\dots\:<{i}_{k}\le\:n}{\left(1-{\prod\:}_{j=1}^{k}{\left({1-\mathfrak{M}}_{ij}^{f}\right)}^{{\mathfrak{f}}_{j}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{{C}_{n}^{k}}}\right)}^{\frac{1}{k}}}\end{array}\right\}\right\}$$\end{document}

Fractional Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS method

In this section, we offer a fractional Diophantine TOPSIS method to address the multi-criteria decision-making problem.

Consider we have m alternatives denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y} = \left\{ {\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}_{1} ,{\text{~}}\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}_{2} ,{\text{~}}\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}_{3} \ldots \quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}_{{\text{m}}} } \right\}$$\end{document} and n criteria i.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:=\left\{{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{1},\:{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{2},\:{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{3}\dots\:{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{\text{n}}\right\}$$\end{document} with unknown criteria weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\mathcal{W}}_{i}=\left({\mathcal{W}}_{1},{\mathcal{W}}_{2},\dots\:,{\mathcal{W}}_{n}\right)}^{T}$$\end{document} such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{W}}_{i}>0$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\sum\:_{i=1}^{n}{\mathcal{W}}_{i}=1$$\end{document}. Let a panel of medical experts \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{ME}^{p},\left(p=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n\right)$$\end{document} with unknown weight vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\varpi\:}_{i}$$\end{document} such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\varpi\:}_{i}>0$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\sum\:_{i=1}^{P}{\varpi\:}_{i}=1,$$\end{document} are invited to evaluate the alternatives according to criteria’s. Here are the key steps of the fractional Diophantine TOPSIS method.

1). Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{ME}^{\left(p\right)}$$\end{document}= \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left( {\wp _{{ij}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } \right)_{{{\text{m}} \times {\text{n}}}}$$\end{document} represent the expert matrix in the form of a fractional Diophantine set as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ME^{{\left( p \right)}} = \left[ {\begin{array}{*{20}c} {U_{1} } & {U_{2} } & {U_{3} } & {U_{n} } \\ {\wp _{{11}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } & {\wp _{{12}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } & \cdots & {\wp _{{1n}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } \\ {\wp _{{21}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } & {\wp _{{22}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } & \cdots & {\wp _{{2n}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } \\ \vdots & \cdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ {\wp _{{m1}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } & {\wp _{{m2}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } & \cdots & {\wp _{{mn}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } \\ \end{array} } \right].$$\end{document}

Where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wp _{{ij}}^{{\left( p \right)}}$$\end{document} represent fractional Diophantine numbers that is

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wp _{{ij}}^{{\left( p \right)}} = \left\{ {{\mathcal{M}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{ij}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} ,{\mathcal{N}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{ij}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} } \right\},\left\{ {{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\wp _{{ij}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} ,{\mathfrak{M}}_{{\wp _{{ij}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} } \right\}.$$\end{document}

2) In decision making typically, criteria are divided into two categories such as cost and benefit criteria. In decision making normalization is not necessary if all of the criteria are of the same type. But in the event that the decision matrix includes both types, it is necessary to change the cost criteria into benefit as follows.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb{N}}^{{\left( p \right)}} = \left\{ {\begin{array}{*{20}c} {\wp _{{ij}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } & {{\text{benefit~type}}} \\ {\left( {\wp _{{ij}}^{{\left( p \right)}} } \right)^{c} } & {{\text{cost~type}}} \\ \end{array} } \right.$$\end{document}.

Determining the weight of experts

The experts in various fields who have cognitive asymmetries in knowledge and experience are requested to assess each alternative against the criteria. In decision making, each expert has weight vectors according to his expertise which are of two types i.e. known weights and unknown weight vectors. It is challenging for experts to make wise decisions when the expert weights are unclear. So it is very important to estimate the weight of each expert which can be computed by the following steps.

3). To determine the weight of experts, we first compute the entropy of each expert matrix.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_{i} \left( \psi \right) = \frac{1}{n}\mathop \sum \limits_{{j = 1}}^{n} \left[ {\left\{ {\sqrt 2 ~cos\pi \left( {\frac{{{\mathcal{M}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} - {\mathcal{N}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} }}{4}} \right) + \sqrt 2 ~cos\pi \left( {\frac{{{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} - {\mathfrak{M}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} }}{4}} \right) - 1} \right\} \times \frac{1}{{\sqrt 2 - 1}}} \right].$$\end{document}

The expert’s weights are obtained by the mathematical equations (a) and (b).

a\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_{j} \left( A \right) = \frac{1}{m}\mathop \sum \limits_{{j = 1}}^{n} E_{i} \left( \psi \right),~j = 1,2, \ldots ,n$$\end{document}

b\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\varpi\:}_{i}=\frac{{E}_{j}\left(A\right)}{{\sum\:}_{j=1}^{n}{E}_{j}\left(A\right)}$$\end{document}

4). After calculating the expert weights, apply the proposed aggregation operators to determine the aggregation matrix.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ME = \left[ {\begin{array}{*{20}c} {U_{1} } & {U_{2} } & {U_{3} } & {U_{n} } \\ {\wp _{{11}} } & {\wp _{{12}} } & \cdots & {\wp _{{1n}} } \\ {\wp _{{21}} } & {\wp _{{22}} } & \cdots & {\wp _{{2n}} } \\ \vdots & \cdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ {\wp _{{m1}} } & {\wp _{{m2}} } & \cdots & {\wp _{{mn}} } \\ \end{array} } \right].$$\end{document}

Analytic hierarchy process (AHP) for weights of attribute

Here, the Analytical Hierarchy Process (AHP) method is used to calculate the criteria weights as it is frequently used to generate criteria weights which makes it extremely helpful and applicable in decision-making situations. The analytical hierarchy process (AHP) is one of the most powerful tools for exploring unweighted information because it offers a clear metric to directly aid decisions by showing the relative relevance of criteria and alternatives. Decisions in the real world often involve a combination of multiple criteria and AHP enables decision-makers to evaluate multiple criteria at the same time, handling complex decision-making more accurately. The following are some useful steps for calculating standard weight information.

5). Assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:Q={\left({q}_{jk}\right)}_{n\times\:n}$$\end{document} be the complementary expert matrix provided by the expert for each criterion given as.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q = \left[ {\begin{array}{*{20}c} {} & {} & {} & {} \\ {q_{{11}} } & {q_{{12}} } & \cdots & {q_{{1n}} } \\ {q_{{21}} } & {q_{{22}} } & \cdots & {q_{{2n}} } \\ \vdots & \cdots & \ddots & \vdots \\ {q_{{m1}} } & {q_{{m2}} } & \cdots & {q_{{mn}} } \\ \end{array} } \right]$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{q}_{jk}$$\end{document} represents the preference degree of criteria Cj over criteria Ck, in the form of a fractional Diophantine set, and where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{q}_{jk}$$\end{document} represents the score value of each entity i.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{q}_{jk}=Sc\left({s}_{{\alpha\:}_{ij}},\:\left({{\overset{\lower0.5em\hbox{$\smash{\scriptscriptstyle\smile}$}}{E} }}_{ij},{\dot{G}}_{ij}\right)\right)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:i=\text{1,2},\dots\:,m,j=\text{1,2},\dots\:,n.$$\end{document} Then the weight vector of criteria is calculated by the following formulas as

c\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{F}}_{j}=\frac{\sum\:_{i}^{m}{q}_{ij}+\left(\raisebox{1ex}{$m$}\!\left/\:\!\raisebox{-1ex}{$2$}\right.\right)-1}{m\left(m-1\right)}$$\end{document}

d\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{w}_{j}=\frac{{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}{\sum\:_{j=1}^{n}{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}$$\end{document}

6). In an aggregated matrix the positive ideal solution (PIS) and negative ideal solution (NIS) are determined by equations (e) and (f).

e\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{j}^{ + } = \left\{ {\mathop {\max }\limits_{{1 \le i \le n}} \left( {Sc\wp _{{ij}} } \right)} \right\}$$\end{document}

And.

f\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{j}^{ - } = \left\{ {\mathop {\max }\limits_{{1 \le i \le n}} \left( {Sc\wp _{{ij}} } \right)} \right\}$$\end{document}

7). Calculate the distance measure between each entity of aggregated matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{u}_{i}$$\end{document} and PIS, NIS by equations (g) and (h). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{\mathcal{M}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} ,{\mathcal{N}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} } \right\},\left\{ {{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} ,{\mathfrak{M}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} ^{{\;\;\;\;p}} } \right\}$$\end{document}

g\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d\left( {u_{i} ,u_{j}^{ + } } \right) = \left[ {\frac{1}{{2n}}\left( {\left| {{\mathcal{M}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} - {\mathcal{M}}_{{\wp _{{\text{j}}} }}^{ + } } \right|^{\lambda } + \left| {{\mathcal{N}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} - {\mathcal{N}}_{{\wp _{{\text{j}}} }}^{ + } } \right|^{\lambda } + \left| {{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} - {\mathfrak{A}}_{{\wp _{{\wp _{j} {\text{j}}}} }}^{ + } } \right|^{\lambda } + \left| {{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} - {\mathfrak{A}}_{{\wp _{{\text{j}}} }}^{ + } } \right|^{\lambda } } \right)} \right]^{{\frac{1}{\lambda }}}$$\end{document}

h\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d\left( {u_{i} ,u_{j}^{ - } } \right) = \left[ {\frac{1}{{2n}}\left( {\left| {{\mathcal{M}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} - {\mathcal{M}}_{{\wp _{{\text{j}}} }}^{ - } } \right|^{\lambda } + \left| {{\mathcal{N}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} - {\mathcal{N}}_{{\wp _{{\text{j}}} }}^{ - } } \right|^{\lambda } + \left| {{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} - {\mathfrak{A}}_{{\wp _{{\wp _{j} {\text{j}}}} }}^{ - } } \right|^{\lambda } + \left| {{\mathfrak{A}}_{{\dot{\wp }_{{{\text{ij}}}} }} - {\mathfrak{A}}_{{\wp _{{\text{j}}} }}^{ - } } \right|^{\lambda } } \right)} \right]^{{\frac{1}{\lambda }}}$$\end{document}

8). Compute the weighted sum of the distance values using the following equations.

i\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}^{+}=\sum\:_{j=1}^{n}{w}_{j}\:d\left({u}_{i},{u}_{j}^{+}\right)$$\end{document}

j\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}^{-}=\sum\:_{j=1}^{n}{w}_{j}\:d\left({u}_{i},{u}_{j}^{-}\right)$$\end{document}

9). Calculate the relative closeness (RC) of the object \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{u}_{i}$$\end{document} by equation (k) and denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{F}}_{i}$$\end{document}.

k\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\mathcal{F}}_{i}=\frac{{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}^{-}}{{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}^{+}+{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}^{-}}$$\end{document}

The graphical representation of the FDF-TOPSIS method is shown in (Fig. 3).

Fig. 3Graph of FDF-TOPSIS method.

Graph of FDF-TOPSIS method.

Numerical application

Cancer is an irregular cell that divides uncontrollably; it begins with genetic changes that cause a cell to rapidly expand and multiply. These cells may eventually spread to other tissues. Genetic factors, the patient’s general health, cancer-specific characteristics, and even the treatment’s intended outcome are among the main variables that affect a comprehensive treatment plan for cancer. To control cancer there are various treatment techniques considered as alternatives such as; radiotherapy, chemotherapy, surgery, hadron therapy, hormone therapy, and immunotherapy2. The detailed descriptions are as follows:

(1) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}}}_{1}:\:$$\end{document}Chemotherapy. Cancer chemotherapy is a process in which cancer cells are killed using anticancer drugs36. These medications are taken intravenously or orally (injected into a vein), entering the bloodstream and reaching cancer cells throughout the body37. This therapy procedure has distinct systemic effects due to its mode of operation. The therapy works by reducing or stopping the rapid cell division and growth of cancer cells. But it also damages rapidly dividing healthy cells, a major drawback of the procedure, but these effects may disappear after treatment is complete. This process is also used in combination with other treatment modalities to ensure greater efficacy and specificity against cancer cells.

(2) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}}}_{2}:\:$$\end{document}Radiotherapy. Radiotherapy is another effective technique for treating local tumors. The intrinsic tumor cells’ radioresistance to therapy, however, causes clinical issues. This technique uses relatively high-energy X-rays to kill tumors or stop their growth38. There are two main approaches to treating cancer namely internal radiation therapy and external beam therapy3. Internal radiation therapy uses radioactive material encapsulated in a delivery device, such as a catheter, seed, needle, or wire placed near or inside the cancer. External beam therapy uses a device outside the body to bombard tumors with radiation.

(3) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}}}_{3}:\:$$\end{document}Hadron Therapy. This is a type of radiotherapy that treats cancer by using (protons, neutrons, and other ions). These particles can penetrate tissues and deposit their peak energy because of their special radiobiological properties. Subatomic particles called hadrons are affected by the powerful nuclear force that holds particles together harmoniously within the nucleus39. Recently, radiotherapy has used protons, neutrons, and light ions as hadrons40. These beams differ from the gamma-ray and X-ray beams used in traditional radiation therapy in terms of their physical and radiobiological characteristics. The dose is distributed throughout the body in a defined pattern due to free interactions between charged hadrons and matter. Light ions accumulate a significant portion of their energy at the end of their path, causing powerful local ionization that is thought to be highly effective against radiation-resistant tumors41.

(4) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}}}_{4}:\:$$\end{document}Hormone therapy. This is another technique (also called endocrine therapy or hormone therapy) that slows or stops the growth of tumors, and hormone receptors or interferes with how cancer cells respond to changes in hormone levels or sensitivity to hormones to stop the body from producing hormone. When tumors lack hormone receptors and are insensitive to hormones, hormone therapy is ineffective or unresponsive. This therapy technique stops the growth of cancer cells by either replacing or inhibiting the action of hormones42.

(5) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}}}_{5}:\:$$\end{document}Immunotherapy. This biological treatment, which helps the body fight disease, involves the use of specific therapeutic agents to improve or revive immune system function. This treatment approach, which is referred to as a “fourth modality” in cancer treatment, consists of three types: monoclonal antibodies (such as trastuzumab), non-specific adjuvant therapy, and immunotherapy, such as interferon or sargramostim, as well as cancer vaccines & other active immunotherapy vaccines, such as the human papillomavirus vaccine43. Individualized immunotherapy with appropriate antibody cocktails could improve the efficacy of immunotherapy. Immunotherapy can be considered an alternative therapy because it can eradicate any remaining tumor cells, regardless of how actively they proliferate44. To successfully induce anticancer immune responses, cancer immunotherapy aims to overcome these obstacles.

(6) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad {\rm A}\!\!\!{\rm Y}}}_{6}:\:$$\end{document}Surgery. The goal of surgery is to remove as much of the tumor as possible.
