There are many different kinds, but the patient will either undergo the procedure of their choice or the one the doctor suggests according to their medical history and type of cancer. This is performed for different reasons, including the relief of advanced-stage symptoms, the removal of additional cancers (across mastectomy or breast-conserving surgery (BSC)), the determination of whether lymph nodes in the armpit have been affected by cancer, the reconstruction of the breast to recover its shape, and the removal of additional cancers. BCS requires the removal of only a portion of the cancerous breast, also known as a lumpectomy, quadrantectomy, partial mastectomy, or segmental mastectomy. Mastectomy is the surgical removal of the entire breast, including nearby tissues. Patients can choose between BCS and mastectomy; the benefits of BCS include the preservation of more breast tissue, but in most cases, additional radiotherapy is required. The majority of mastectomy procedures, on the other hand, don’t tend to involve additional radiotherapy. The goal of this procedure is to determine if the lymph node has been affected by the cancer. This is crucial because it aids in determining the cancer’s stage. Most patients think about having their breasts rebuilt to improve their appearance after surgery (breast reconstruction). Surgery can still be useful in slowing the spread of the cancer or to prevent or relieve its symptoms, even though it is unlikely to be able to cure breast cancer that has spread to other parts of the body45.

To evaluate the best cancer treatment process there are various factors (criteria), such as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{1}=$$\end{document} Treatment duration, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{2}=$$\end{document} Cost of treatment, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{3}$$\end{document}= Comfortability, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{4}=\:$$\end{document}Side effects, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\text{\quad \d{U'}}\:}_{5}=\:$$\end{document}Satisfaction which play significant roles in the treatment’s course and are therefore required to examine the various treatment. The graphical representation of anticancer therapies is as follows in (Fig. 4), and the stepwise decision-making process is given in the next section.

Fig. 4Graphically representation of anticancer therapies.

Graphically representation of anticancer therapies.

Comparative analysis

In this section, we check the applicability, validity, and effectiveness of our proposed decision-making model by comparing it with the existing MCDM techniques. The suggested technique handles values in a set of FDFSs along with weighted Muirhead mean operators. Moreover, we employed the TOPSIS approach to choose the best alternatives (options) from a limited set of alternatives (options). To confirm the effectiveness and flexibility of the proposed method, we consider the same expert data and construct three decision matrices in the context of FDFNs which consist a set of six alternatives and a set of possible five attributes and we shall utilize with other aggregation operators, such as FS operator4, IFS5, PyFSs8, q-ROFs10, LDFWA17, LDFOWA17, LDFWG17, LDFOWG17, q-RLDFWA20, q-RLDFOWA20, GRA Method46, EDAS Method47 and WASPAS Method48 as shown in (Table 9).

Reasons and background

Most decision-making processes are complicated by the presence of incomplete and inaccurate information making the decision process more difficult. Therefore, by introducing this method we aim to provide a more robust framework that can accommodate such complexities and uncertainties.

The reason and motivation for using this strategy is its ability to provide more comprehensive and adaptive solutions than traditional methods. Although our focus is on the selection of the best treatment process, it is worth emphasizing that the utility of this strategy extends beyond this specific domain. The selection of the best treatment process is considered a type of DM problem that involves multiple criteria. However, the decision to employ this approach should be driven by the nature of the problem and the characteristics of the data. In instances where complex, ambiguous, and multi-criteria evaluations are required, our method provides an effective alternative to traditional decision-making techniques.

There are some reasons why we proposed the TOPSIS method.

In real-life medical decision-making, there are many factors involved that need to be handled with more flexibility than other fuzzy sets. Therefore, the fractional Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS methods are powerful and can model more accurately than traditional fuzzy sets to select cancer treatments by considering the management of uncertainty in clinical data.One of the main reasons why the TOPSIS method was proposed is that it can evaluate and rank options based on multiple criteria. In the real world, decision-makers often have to consider multiple criteria or aspects. TOPSIS enables decision-makers to evaluate options while taking into account many, sometimes conflicting, factors.In situations that require complex decisions, such as choosing a cancer treatment, the Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS method performs better and also addresses significant flaws such as lack of flexibility, inability to handle ambiguity, and overly simplistic presentation of knowledge.

In real-life medical decision-making, there are many factors involved that need to be handled with more flexibility than other fuzzy sets. Therefore, the fractional Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS methods are powerful and can model more accurately than traditional fuzzy sets to select cancer treatments by considering the management of uncertainty in clinical data.

In real-life medical decision-making, there are many factors involved that need to be handled with more flexibility than other fuzzy sets. Therefore, the fractional Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS methods are powerful and can model more accurately than traditional fuzzy sets to select cancer treatments by considering the management of uncertainty in clinical data.

One of the main reasons why the TOPSIS method was proposed is that it can evaluate and rank options based on multiple criteria. In the real world, decision-makers often have to consider multiple criteria or aspects. TOPSIS enables decision-makers to evaluate options while taking into account many, sometimes conflicting, factors.

One of the main reasons why the TOPSIS method was proposed is that it can evaluate and rank options based on multiple criteria. In the real world, decision-makers often have to consider multiple criteria or aspects. TOPSIS enables decision-makers to evaluate options while taking into account many, sometimes conflicting, factors.

In situations that require complex decisions, such as choosing a cancer treatment, the Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS method performs better and also addresses significant flaws such as lack of flexibility, inability to handle ambiguity, and overly simplistic presentation of knowledge.

In situations that require complex decisions, such as choosing a cancer treatment, the Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS method performs better and also addresses significant flaws such as lack of flexibility, inability to handle ambiguity, and overly simplistic presentation of knowledge.

Significance of the study in evolutionary economics and organizational behavior

The proposed fractional Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS method can significantly increase the impact of research, especially in fields such as evolutionary economics and organizational behavior.

Utilizing the proposed method with evolutionary economics

The study of evolutionary economics focuses on how economic systems change over time, emphasizing how competition, innovation, and adaptation affect economic outcomes. Due to its ability to manage uncertainty and fuzzy data, the fractional Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS method can be used to represent decision-making processes in dynamically changing economic environments. This approach can be used to help with decisions about resource allocation, innovation, and technology adoption all important topics in evolutionary economics.

Utilizing the proposed method in organizational behavior

The study of organizational behavior covers decision-making, leadership, conflict resolution, and organizational transformation, as well as how individuals and groups behave within organizations. Since the fractional Diophantine fuzzy TOPSIS method focuses on prioritizing options, it has wide applications in organizational behavior. Organizations often face decisions involving multiple criteria. This method provides a systematic approach to evaluating these options, taking into account both the subjective opinions of stakeholders and objective data. This method has been used in evolutionary economics and can also be applied to organizational behavior to evaluate different leadership styles, strategic choices, and even organizational culture change.

Conclusion

This paper presents a decision-making technique to select cancer treatment processes. In practical decision-making situations, the fractional Diophantine fuzzy set provides a large space for choosing membership, non-membership, and reference parameters. Therefore, this paper developed a new FDFS concept which is more efficient due to the condition of the reference parameters. As FDFS is an adaptable and simple tool that allows people to freely represent information, we developed some useful Muirhead aggregation operators for FDFS. Finally, we developed the FDFS-TOPSIS method and also developed an entropy measure to find unknown weight vectors. The actual medical issue was used to further explain the suggested approach. Finally, a comparative analysis of the proposed technique with competing methods was conducted to test the validity and feasibility of the proposed technique, and the proposed approach was found to be more stable than all competing methods and provide an effective support tool for decision-making.

Limitation and future direction

This study proposed fractional Diophantine fuzzy aggregation operators, which is a useful tool for dealing with decision problems, but can only handle one-dimensional data. This study cannot handle multiple cases with two-dimensional information. To overcome the shortcomings of the proposed work, this work can be further extended to develop complex fractional Diophantine fuzzy set and several aggregation operators such as Hamacher, Dombi, Choquet integral, Einstein, and interaction aggregation operators. Furthermore, we will apply the invented method to other fields, including artificial neural networks, and extend it to other generalizations of fuzzy sets.
